OTHER AGENT (CLAUDE) SAID:
# Round 8: candle ingestion, as implemented vs. the pending local-candle direction

I didn't change any source, config, database, git state or runtime, and I didn't touch production. Plan mode is on, but this session has no file-writing or plan-exit tool, so this report is the only output.

## FINDINGS

### Corrections to my round-7 claims

**Correction A: "a signal row alone counts as analysed" (my New 3) was overstated. I withdraw it as a defect.**
- It's true that `missing()` treats any `trade_signal` row as done (`LocalClosedCandleDetector.java:137`).
- But `AnalysisService.analyzeForProcessing` is `@Transactional`. It saves the signal and calls `processingStore.register(...)` in the same transaction (`AnalysisService.java:109-115`), and `register` calls `requireTransaction()` (`SignalProcessingStore.java:38-39`).
- So a live signal and its durable `signal_processing_work` row are saved together or not at all. If `processSignal` then throws, the work row remains and `SignalProcessingRecovery.recover()` handles it (`SignalProcessingRecovery.java:19-41`).
- The real follow-on issue is different (New A below).

**Correction B: the `closeTime` convention behind my New 1 is now confirmed in this repo.**
- Reconciliation builds `closeTime = open + interval − 1 ms` (`LocalCandleReconciliationService.java:155`).
- It then requires an exact `k.closeTime().equals(candidate.closeTime)` match against Binance (`:166`).
- So the analysis in New 1 holds. A candidate always has `closeTime + lookback ≥ now − 1 ms`, so the expiry test `!retryDeadline.isAfter(now)` (`:97-99`, `:111-113`) is true only within about 1 ms. Expiry comes in practice only from the attempt count (`attempts>6`).
- **Added nuance:** the backoff delays for attempts 1–6 add up to about 122 s (`retryDelaySeconds`, `:129`). The 1m lookback is `max(180, 90×2)=180` s (`:47`; `SharedEventPolicy.java:26`). So reaching 7 counted attempts inside the window is only just possible, and only if the lane is idle at every retry. A lane that is busy at each due time (`:46` `continue` doesn't count an attempt) ages the candle out with no outcome.

**Correction C: I overstated the claude-docs OBSERVE point.**
- `claude-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:45-50` puts "LIVE / OBSERVE" under one heading. But it correctly says direct ingestion is disabled "in LIVE".
- Its defect is omission and ambiguity, not a false statement. It never says that OBSERVE runs both paths, and "The two paths must never run together" is not true of OBSERVE.

### New findings this round

**New A: interrupted processing is parked in `REVIEW_REQUIRED` with no automatic replay.**
- `SignalProcessingStore.quarantineInterrupted` moves `RUNNING` work older than 300 s to `REVIEW_REQUIRED`, with the message "Interrupted processing requires review; no automatic replay" (`SignalProcessingStore.java:161-171`).
- `recover()` logs "no automatic replay" (`SignalProcessingRecovery.java:22-23`).
- AGENTS.md allows this kind of quarantine to be concluded automatically only from `EFFECT_COMMITTED` or `EFFECT_ABSENT` evidence, and says unknown atomicity is a release blocker. I found no code here that concludes it automatically.
- In OFF mode, the detector sees the work row and treats the candle as done (`:137`). Nothing then drives this work to an automatic terminal outcome.
- This conflicts with AGENTS.md's automation direction ("no pending … outcome may require manual approval"). I did not trace whether some other component turns `REVIEW_REQUIRED` into a terminal state.

**New B: OBSERVE mode can run STARTUP trades from backfilled candles.** This strengthens the OBSERVE objection beyond "dual ingestion".
- In `MarketDataBootstrapService.bootstrap`, OFF returns right after the import (`:107-110`). LIVE never gets there (`:50`, `:73`).
- OBSERVE (`!LIVE && !OFF`) continues on to `analyzeForProcessing(..., STARTUP)` and `paperTradingService.processSignal(signal)` (`:135-136`).
- So OBSERVE turns a REST/BACKFILL import into an executable paper signal at startup and on coin activation. That is the exact behaviour FIX-153 forbids in OFF (comment at `:105-106`).

**New C: the import/WebSocket race cannot happen at startup, only at runtime.** This narrows Codex's point 5.
- The bootstrap is an `ApplicationRunner` (`MarketDataBootstrapService.java:30`), and Spring Boot runs runners before it publishes `ApplicationReadyEvent`.
- The WebSocket starts on `ApplicationReadyEvent` (`BinanceWebSocketManager.java:95-104`).
- So startup bootstrap finishes before the stream exists (inferred from Spring Boot's lifecycle order; not tested here).
- The race needs a runtime import: the `/api/market-data/import` endpoint or `bootstrapSymbol` during coin activation (`:72-92`).
- The window opens when a kline's `closeTime ≤ exchangeNow` (`MarketDataService.java:37,45`) but the WebSocket's final close for that candle hasn't committed yet. Usually that is sub-second to a few seconds. During a WebSocket outage it lasts the whole outage.

### Rechecked against code and still standing
- **Mode guards:** only LIVE disables direct ingestion. The SpEL `== 'LIVE'` guard is at `BinanceWebSocketManager.java:28`, `MarketDataService.java:19` and `MarketDataBootstrapService.java:31`. In OBSERVE, `SharedMarketConsumer` runs, since it only short-circuits on OFF (`:136,142,323,391`).
- **Ordinary import uses the BACKFILL duplicate-key update.** It closes an open runtime row (`CandleRepository.java:99-106, 173-188`) without changing provenance or token, and writes no audit or outcome. The comment at `:205` ("backfill inserts missing identities only") is contradicted by the SQL directly above it.
- **The candle closed by that race is unrecoverable by every path.**
  - The detector requires a token (`LocalClosedCandleDetector.java:50`).
  - Reconciliation requires `closed=0` (`LocalCandleReconciliationService.java:131`).
  - Gap detection skips identities that are present (`:150-155`).
- **Reconciliation:**
  - It runs only when enabled and in OFF mode (`:93`).
  - Its open-candidate query applies `LIMIT 50` per interval across all symbols, with no enabled-symbol filter, before the `audit.due` and `positiveLossEvidence` filters (`:129-140`).
  - Its gap discovery does filter to enabled symbols (`:146`).
  - It is off by default (`application.yml`, established in round 7; not re-read this round).
- **Historical-only work:** the worker's historical branch (`CandleClosedAnalysisWorker.java:160-166`) is in the same class that holds `PaperTradingService` (`:37`, used at `:101` and `:175`). This is separation by branch, not structural separation.
- **Replay:**
  - The `candles()` loader has no `closed` filter (`RegressionTestWorker.java:509-512`).
  - The dataset loader filters for closed candles (`:475-480`).
  - Neither models provenance, close tokens, reconciliation audit, grace timing or the detector.

### Challenging Codex
- **Point 2/OBSERVE:** correct but incomplete. It misses New B: OBSERVE also *executes* STARTUP signals from imports.
- **Point 5:** correct on mechanism. It doesn't say that startup is excluded by lifecycle order (New C), which narrows the trigger set.
- **Point 8:** misses the expiry arithmetic (New 1/Correction B) and the busy-lane non-counting.
- **Its documentation-contradiction point** says claude-docs "groups LIVE and OBSERVE as shared-only". That's slightly overstated; see Correction C.
- **Codex missed New A** (interrupted work parked in `REVIEW_REQUIRED`).

## OBJECTIONS (unresolved, material)
1. **Ordinary import** (endpoint or coin activation) can finalise an open runtime candle with no grace period, audit or outcome. No path recovers that candle afterwards. The comment at `CandleRepository.java:205` is false.
2. **OBSERVE** runs direct ingestion alongside the shared consumer, and it executes STARTUP paper signals derived from imports (New B).
3. **The detector cannot guarantee a terminal outcome.** Deadline-based expiry is effectively dead code. A busy lane or a restart (in-memory `retries`/`analysedUntil`, `:28-29`) lets a tokened candle age out silently.
4. **Interrupted signal work** goes to `REVIEW_REQUIRED` with no automatic conclusion (New A). This conflicts with the automation direction unless an effect-evidence resolver exists. I didn't find one.
5. **Reconciliation:**
   - It is off by default.
   - `LIMIT 50` is applied before filtering, with no symbol filter on open candidates.
   - It has no shared Binance 429/418 rate-limit or ban handling (round 7; not re-read this round).
6. **Historical-only isolation** is a branch, not structural.
7. **Replay parity** for ingestion, recovery and admission is not shown.
8. **Documentation drift:**
   - AGENTS.md and `claude-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:35-37` both still call V103 an unconditional fresh-install blocker. B105 claims to resolve it, but that is untested.
   - claude-docs is ambiguous about OBSERVE.

YOUR PRIOR FINDINGS:
## FINDINGS

1. The checked-in default is trader-owned ingestion: `shared-market.mode=OFF`. In this mode the trader opens Binance kline WebSockets, performs REST bootstrap/imports, and persists to local `crypto_ai.candle`.

2. Collector/shared-market ownership remains implemented, not removed. In `LIVE`, the trader connects read-only to `crypto_ai_v2` and shared executors consume collector stream/cursor tables. `OBSERVE` also creates that external collector connection and starts shared clocks.

3. `OBSERVE` is architecturally contradictory: direct WebSocket/REST ingestion is disabled only for `LIVE`, while shared executors run in every non-`OFF` mode. Thus OBSERVE runs both direct ingestion and shared-market processing, contrary to AGENTS.md’s pending rule that they must not run together.

4. Runtime WebSocket candle updates are monotonic: completed candles are not overwritten; a transition token is assigned only on the SQL open-to-closed transition. The transaction order is input → protection → observer → close-event publication; dispatch occurs after commit.

5. Ordinary REST import/backfill is unsafe relative to the pending design. It calls a generic `BACKFILL` duplicate-key upsert. Against an existing open runtime row, it updates the OHLCV data and changes `closed` to `1`, but does not create a runtime close token, dispatch analysis, or write reconciliation/outcome evidence. The nearby comment that backfill “inserts missing identities only” contradicts the SQL.

6. Therefore, the reported live-close interleaving is substantiated by code: REST can close an open runtime row first; a later final WebSocket event cannot install its token because `closed=1`; normal close dispatch does not occur. Reconciliation subsequently excludes that row because it only selects `closed=0`; the local detector requires a token. This is a material admission gap.

7. A dedicated reconciliation implementation exists and is substantially closer to the pending direction: it uses exchange time plus grace, update-only recovery for an existing open candle, insert-if-absent for gaps, audit/outcome records, and no live analysis dispatch. However, it is disabled by default.

8. Even when enabled, reconciliation is incomplete against AGENTS.md requirements: the open-candidate query limits rows before the due/loss-evidence filters; reconnect evidence is process-local; no demonstrated shared Binance 429/418/ban admission control exists across reconciliation, bootstrap, clock sync, and other REST callers.

9. Dispatch admission is not fully durable. The dispatcher can replace pending 1m work or reject higher-timeframe work under bounded queue pressure. The detector can rediscover tokened candles, but only while the lane is idle and within a bounded lookback. Its retry state is in memory; a restart or prolonged busy lane can let an uncompleted candle age out without a durable terminal outcome.

10. The detector’s `missing()` considers an existing `trade_signal` sufficient completion even if subsequent signal processing fails. The worker persists the signal before `paperTradingService.processSignal`; its general failure path returns `REVIEW_REQUIRED`. The code does not establish that every such local failure has terminal durable work/outcome evidence.

11. Historical-only recovery is behaviorally separated but not structurally isolated: the same `CandleClosedAnalysisWorker` that selects `analyzeRecovered` also injects and invokes `PaperTradingService` for live processing. This fails the stronger AGENTS.md requirement that historical-only paths be structurally unable to reach execution services.

12. Replay does not demonstrate ingestion/recovery parity. The normal replay loader does not filter `closed`; the dataset forward path does. Neither shown path simulates close tokens, provenance, reconciliation grace/retries/audits, dispatcher saturation, or recovery admission.

13. Production behavior is unknown. This was a repository-only review; no production systems, credentials, databases, runtime processes, deployed overrides, or logs were accessed.

## Trade investigations
When asked to investigate a trade:

- Confirm the application, environment, symbol, and trade ID.
- Use configured database and SSH connections without displaying secrets.
- Use read-only, bounded queries and targeted log searches.
- Read the actual schema before choosing tables or columns.
- Reconstruct the timeline: candle, signal, decision, entry, protection,
  and exit. Display timestamps in KSA (UTC+03:00), interpreting stored
  timestamps as UTC.
- Trace the relevant code and configuration for each decision.
- Check data freshness, entry quality, strategy, risk/reward, fees,
  stop-loss, and exit reason where applicable.
- Assess the entry using only information available at that moment.
  Use subsequent price action separately to evaluate the outcome.
- Distinguish a valid losing trade from a logic defect, stale data,
  execution delay, configuration issue, or missing evidence.
- Check whether the behavior can be reproduced in Replay.
- Support findings with record IDs, log timestamps, and code references.
- State uncertainties; do not invent a cause.
- Do not change code, configuration, services, or production data during
  an investigation unless the user requests a fix.

Report:
1. What happened.
2. Why the system entered and exited.
3. Whether the behavior followed the intended rules.
4. Confirmed defects and missing evidence.
5. Recommended correction and regression checks, if needed.

## Current architecture direction (pending implementation)

The current priority is to remove the trader's runtime dependency on the
collector. This is a design decision, not an implemented/deployed change.

- Target runtime: trader-owned Binance kline ingestion and local
  `crypto_ai.candle` writes with `shared-market.mode=OFF`.
- The collector remains independent in `crypto_ai_v2`; the trader must not
  connect to, read from, or write collector stream/cursor tables at runtime.
- Do not run direct ingestion and the shared-market executors together.
- Applied shared-market tables and Flyway history are historical evidence; do
  not drop, reset, or edit them merely to enable the new path.
- `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` still requires
  `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event` for a fresh Flyway install. Treat
  trader-only bootstrap as a release blocker until a forward, tested installer
  policy exists. Never edit an applied Flyway migration.

## Local-candle implementation safety

When implementing or reviewing the local-candle design:

- Keep `shared-market.fix142.enabled=false`,
  `shared-market.fix142.acknowledgment-enabled=false`, and scheduled analysis
  disabled for the OFF-mode cutover unless a reviewed change explicitly says
  otherwise.
- Preserve the input -> protection -> observer -> close-dispatch transaction
  order. Candle close analysis must not run on the Binance ingestion thread.
- A closed candle needs durable/recoverable analysis admission; an in-memory
  event/queue alone loses work across a crash. Recovery must distinguish fresh
  execution-capable work from historical-only analysis.
- Candle updates must be monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or
  overwrite a closed candle. Publish close work only for an atomic
  open-to-closed transition.
- A WebSocket reconnect can occur at a candle-close boundary and Binance may
  not replay that final closed-kline event. Candle recovery is automatic: use
  an exact authoritative Binance REST candle only after exchange-time
  finality and the measured live-close grace. It may either finalise a matching
  existing `closed=0` row or insert a wholly absent identity inside the active
  data-quality window. A recovered open row preserves provenance; an inserted
  gap row uses `BACKFILL` provenance. Neither path may alter a completed row,
  create a close-transition token, dispatch analysis, create a live signal,
  or execute a trade. The recovery must have durable retry/audit evidence,
  bounded rate-limit handling, health visibility, and no manual repair step.
- Automation direction: no pending candle or analysis outcome may require
  manual approval, manual database repair, or a persisted trading fence.
  Reconciliation must retry and resolve automatically, or age out to a durable
  explicit outcome with a visible reason. This does not permit unverified data
  to masquerade as valid or force an entry: a genuine strategy/risk veto or
  unavailable authoritative data remains non-executable evidence, not a
  silently stuck pending signal.
- Gap backfill must be a pure insert-if-absent operation. It must never use a
  duplicate-key update that could finalise or alter a live row which appeared
  while the authoritative REST request was in progress. Recovery candidates
  are bounded by the active quality window and terminal audit outcomes are not
  retried. Each candle/work path must write one durable terminal analysis
  outcome; transient failures remain retryable and write no terminal outcome.
- Any future universal trading-mutation barrier is an in-memory drain
  participant only. Outside an active drain it must not block, delay, queue,
  or persistently fence fresh trading, signals, or protection. It may reject
  new mutations only while draining and must then wait for admitted mutations
  to finish before shutdown.
- Historical-only recovery must be structurally unable to reach execution,
  wallet, position, order, or executable-work services. Quarantined work with
  possible financial effects may be concluded automatically only from proven
  durable effect evidence (`EFFECT_COMMITTED` or `EFFECT_ABSENT`); unknown
  atomicity remains a release blocker. Binance REST rate-limit/ban handling
  must be shared by reconciliation, clock sync, bootstrap, and other REST
  callers.
- Enforce freshness from persisted candle close time before analysis and again
  before execution. Historical-only signals must be explicitly marked and
  excluded from live-latency monitoring.
- Price protection must receive the Binance observation timestamp and validate
  age/order inside the protection transaction. Paper-trading effects and their
  accepted price-event state must commit atomically; any future live-exchange
  effect needs a separate idempotent design.
- Do not add manual approval, persisted review fences, or database locks that
  block new analysis, signals, entries, or protection unless the user changes
  that direction. In-process coordination and normal database constraints are
  distinct from persisted trading fences.
- OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must not query or write retired shared-market
  tables. Existing `REVIEW_REQUIRED` history must not block OFF-mode work.
- Keep production and Replay evidence aligned. Replay must fail a parity claim
  for unresolved direct price-event evidence, while deliberate exclusions stay
  excluded.

## Delivery discipline

- Treat the local-candle architecture as not deployment-ready until its
  migration/bootstrap policy, concurrency, crash/restart, stale-input,
  Replay-parity, and Java 21/MySQL validation gates pass.
- Do not deploy or change production configuration/data unless the user
  explicitly requests it.
- Protection coverage for open positions during application replacement remains
  an unresolved release gate. Do not represent the local-candle design as
  deployment-ready until it is implemented or conclusively disproven.

## FIX-154: pending higher-timeframe BUY validation

FIX-154 is a separate, not-yet-implemented design. Do not merge it into the
local-candle change or describe it as deployed.

Purpose: a historical bearish 1h signal may block or delay a current 1m BUY
only when its own evidence is fresh, data-quality-valid, and sufficiently
strong. A historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` must remain investigatory
evidence, not a higher-timeframe validity gate; the current 1m signal's own
BTC protection remains unchanged.

- Score usable bearish 1h evidence into `NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`, or
  `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE` using closed-candle local indicators.
- A hard veto requires strong 1h evidence plus fresh, quality-valid bearish
  5m confirmation. Stale, invalid, or unknown historical quality can never
  hard-veto.
- Persist `trade_signal.data_quality_valid` from
  `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new signal. Historical NULL
  means unknown/invalid; never backfill it to true.
- A confirmed 1h recovery may release only a soft conflict and only with fresh,
  quality-valid, non-bearish 5m evidence.
- Preserve the legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` behavior: the new data-quality gate
  applies to the new hard-veto/recovery paths, not that legacy fallback.
- Add immutable BUY-evaluation audit evidence; maintain Replay parity and
  prevent future-candle or wall-clock look-ahead.
